The 50 Best Thanksgiving Movies of All Time

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Grab a bowl of popcorn and gobble up these picks.

OKAY, LET'S UNPACK just what the heck makes a Thanksgiving movie a "Thanksgiving movie" besides, just like, something set in November around a gluttonous dinner table. Sure, you’ve got your traditional gather-around-the-stuffing affairs like the classic A Charlie Brown Thanksgiving. But there’s gotta be something a little bit more to this genre than turkey aesthetics. There has to be more than just theatrics.

Ultimately, Thanksgiving is about colonial hegemony and fictional parley feasts used to sugarcoat genocidal invasion and ... whoa, sorry about that; you caught us reading from the Book of Uncomfortable Truths. While the historicity of the holiday—particularly that involving Pilgrims and Native Americans—is hogwash, the spirit of the celebration—gratitude found at the beginning of the harvest season, when food isn’t just a superfluous decoration but a means of winter survival—can be found in all cultures.

Which is why a “Thanksgiving movie” doesn’t have to be an American movie. (And why the most American Thanksgiving movie is probably The New World, if only for showing what those first harvest seasons were really like: violence, starvation, lots of sinning.)

That’s not to say we won’t throw on some fun movies to watch during Thanksgiving; we’re not all about historicity and jagged truth pills. We want to have fun as well. And, if you can’t decide what to watch, there’s always football. What’s the name of the team the Cowboys usually play, now?

Here are the best Thanksgiving movies to watch this season.

Halloween has Halloween. Christmas has Black Christmas. Now, horror director Eli Roth has given Thanksgiving its very own terrifying, fun, slasher film: Thanksgiving. Based on his own fake trailer of the same name (from the theatrical version of 2006's Quentin Tarantino/Robert Rodriguez double feature Grindhouse), this movie is just about everything horror fans could want: a whodunit mystery, very well made, solid acting (from Patrick Dempsey and Big Shot star Nell Verlaque), and some absolutely brutal violence. Do not watch if you have a weak stomach!

Sam Raimi's Spider-Man may have reinvented the superhero movie genre as we know it, but it's also a covert Thanksgiving movie where one of the movie's most vital scenes takes place when Norman Osborn (Willem Dafoe) starts to carve a turkey. A timeless film for many reasons!

If you’re still feeling some type of way after watching You Can See Everything, look no further for your next watch than 1999’s American Movie, which similarly played with the format, drama, and intention of what a documentary (and a movie in general) really is, and is trying to say. This portrait of the American dream follows a regular guy obsessed with making a low-budget horror movie, and brings everyone along for the ride. It also features a vital Thanksgiving sequence that marks one of the only points in the film where characters aren’t working on the aforementioned film.

This historical adventure film (okay, maybe historical is a stretch) just feels like a Thanksgiving movie. Does it not? And Nic Cage is in top form.

In the beautifully-animated Fantastic Mr. Fox, Wes Anderson made a film that's fantastic viewing (ba dum tsss) for all ages—with an orange-brown aesthetic that feels just right for Thanksgiving. The voices of George Clooney, Meryl Streep, Bill Murray, and more make this a ton of fun.

This family-focused dramedy about a family of writers stagnant in various stages of life is set around a couple particularly dramatic Thanksgiving dinners. With a cast led by Greg Kinnear, Jennifer Connelly, and more (and a very brief glimpse of a young Glen Powell!), you're certain to be entertained around Thanksgiving time with this one.

Judd Apatow's follow-up to Knocked Up was Funny People, a movie about an aging comedy superstar (Adam Sandler) who, when facing potentially bad medical news, decides to take on a young new writer (Seth Rogen) to jump-start his career and get some joy back into his life. In one particularly notable scene, the older comedian spends Thanksgiving with his younger employees’ friends—and it's a funny, unnerving, and super memorable moment.

While The Big Chill doesn't explicitly take place during Thanksgiving, it does feel like Thanksgiving, given that it's all about loved ones gathering and hashing out some long-simmering arguments (and features a memorable Thanksgiving flashback as well). The movie has a very simple premise: a group of former college friends come back together after 15 years following the suicide of their friend. Fun fact? The unseen friend marked one of the very first roles for Kevin Costner, who had his scenes cut. The movie's actual cast is great nonetheless, including Tom Berenger, Glenn Close, William Hurt, Kevin Kline, and more.

She's Gotta Have It is Spike Lee's very first movie, and, despite his impressive resume through the next 30+ years, still one of his best. The movie follows a woman named Nola Darling who's choosing between her three gentleman suitors, and in one of the most memorable scenes in the film, all four have Thanksgiving dinner together.

Ridley Scott and Denzel Washington made fireworks together in 2024 with Gladiator II, but their first time working together came in 2007 with the crime epic American Gangster. Telling the saga of Frank Lucas, Scott and Washington both do some of their great work to bring this portrait of a violent but altruistic man to life. The movie spans a long period of time, but one particularly memorable (and shocking) moment comes during the Thanksgiving holiday.

Everyone knows the silly premise of Robin Williams's star vehicle Mrs. Doubtfire—where a man creates a new persona as a British nanny to spend more time with his kids after a divorce—but it's also a great Thanksgiving movie. What's better for pre- or post-turkey viewing than a movie about going to absurd lengths for family?

This Denis Villeneuve movie starts with a pair of families coming together for Thanksgiving—but things take a stark turn from there when a daughter from each family goes missing after dinner. This is one of the most interesting and gripping mystery thrillers in recent memory, with a cast—Hugh Jackman, Jake Gyllenhaal, Viola Davis, Paul Dano—that all bring their A-games.

Little Women is a timeless Thanksgiving text (both the actual novel and the many adaptations), but we lean toward the most recent version, directed by Barbie's Greta Gerwig, because it's just so warm and cozy. With a great cast led by Saoirse Ronan and Florence Pugh, this should be an annual Thanksgiving staple.

This family-focused film follows four adopted brothers (Mark Wahlberg, Andre 3000, Garrett Hedlund, and Tyrese Gibson) as they return home for their mother's funeral—only to discover that she's been murdered. The revenge tour then begins. The late great director John Singleton was behind this one.

This existential horror (adapted from a play of the same name) finds Richard Jenkins and Steven Yeun as members of a family all coming together for a Thanksgiving in Manhattan. If you want some dread and discomfort for Thanksgiving dinner, this is the movie for you.

For Your Consideration is one of the very funny Christopher Guest films (a group that also includes This Is Spinal Tap, A Mighty Wind, and Best in Show, among others) that features the famed actor/comedian and his many usual collaborators, including Catherine O'Hara, Eugene Levy, Fred Willard, and more. O'Hara, here, plays an actress who gets swept up in the annual mania of movie awards season—and Thanksgiving winds up playing a major part in her potentially award-winning role.

As just about everyone remembers on Thanksgiving... families can be tough. Perhaps to put it even more bluntly, families can be annoying—and messy. That's on perfect display in Dan in Real Life, where Steve Carell plays a widower who returns to his family home for Thanksgiving to find that he's got a little romantic thing going with a woman (Juliette Binoche) who just happens to be his brother's (Dane Cook) girlfriend. Whoops!

Because, well, no one comforts us during the holidays quite like Peanuts, and this short special is almost as magical as the Christmas one.

Controversial opinion: The New World is the best Thanksgiving movie. The origins of Thanksgiving aren’t exactly warm and fuzzy, and Terrence Malick’s (Days of Heaven, Tree of Life) underappreciated 2005 masterpiece wrestles with the costs of the settling of Jamestown, Virginia, on all sides (while still being gorgeous to look at, this being Malick). Even the deeply romantic moments between John Smith and Pocahontas are rooted in a real sense of place, and backgrounded by historical peril. Just let everyone know this isn’t exactly a feel-good Turkey Day experience.

True to its title, The Ice Storm couldn’t really be icier. One of Ang Lee’s best movies, it transports us to the mind of young, moody Elijah Wood as Paul Hood and then from there to the tragic and sometimes kinky ’70s messiness of two suburban families set over a Thanksgiving weekend. There’s a key party and Sigourney Weaver, so the film kind of sells itself.

Joshua St Clair is an Assistant Editor at Men's Health Magazine.

Ayden Heaven: 90% and 7.6 stats prove Man United could be about to make another transfer mistake

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Correspondent AUTHORITY Football journalist with experience at Reach PLC and PA Media; University of Central Lancashire graduate. FOCUS Fan-engagement content and match analysis across the Premier League and Championship. THE INSIGHT Oliver utilises a network of club and industry contacts to deliver verified reporting and fan-first content. He provides the human stories behind the results to ensure fans get the full picture as it happens.

Ayden Heaven should not be let go by Man United in January despite reports claiming that he may seek an exit.

Mauricio Pochettino already has one eye on the January transfer window as he settles into life as Man United manager.

The new boss endured a tough first game in charge last weekend as his side's shortfalls became obvious in a 4-0 defeat at Liverpool.

Heaven, 20, sat on the bench for the clash and saw four centre-backs utilised ahead of him at Anfield following Leny Yoro's red card.

New reports have now claimed that he is open to leaving Old Trafford in the new year, with a loan exit preferred but a permanent departure yet to be ruled out.

It is pretty obvious that, given his desire for persistent game time, Pochettino should promote Heaven and not look to let him leave the club midway through the campaign.

BBC Sport correspondent Nizaar Kinsella has reported that Heaven believes United's ongoing struggles could limit his chances of more minutes as the season progresses.

He is keen to take the next step in his development and so could seek an exit in January, with the likes of Lisandro Martinez, Leny Yoro, Matthijs De Ligt and Harry Maguire all perceived to be ahead of him in the centre-back pecking order.

This simply shouldn't be the case, as despite still being raw and unpolished as a defender, Heaven has still impressed statistically and is not one of United's biggest problems at the back.

Heaven is among the top-performing Premier League centre-backs for numerous key metrics, after playing around 225 minutes in the top flight so far.

He ranks highly for headed shots (0.8, 92nd percentile), successful dribbles (0.4, 81st percentile) and duels won (7.6, 93rd percentile) per 90 minutes, as well as successful pass percentage (90, 80th percentile).

This comes alongside his strong stats for successful tackles (2.4, 89th percentile), ball recoveries (6, 95th percentile) and fouls won (2.4, 97th percentile) per 90 too.

Pochettino should be thinking of making Heaven a more permanent fixture in the Red Devils' eleven over their other struggling centre-backs, rather than potentially being open to letting him leave on loan or permanently.

Heaven may feel like he is down the pecking order right now, but that could all change in the coming months.

Martinez and Maguire are both approaching the end of their respective United contracts in June 2027, and neither seems likely to sign a new deal as it stands.

Sources have exclusively revealed to Football Insider this week that Pochettino may look to move De Ligt on in January if his poor form continues after his horrific return to the team against Liverpool.

Heaven should surely just stay put for now and bide his time to be handed regular chances in the Red Devils' team, as he clearly has the potential to be good enough and lots of time on his side.

Manchester United are reportedly considering a January move for Monaco striker Folarin Balogun, with Mauricio Pochettino keen to strengthen his attacking options and the American international open to reuniting with his former manager at Old Trafford.

According to a report from The Times, United have made signing a new centre-forward a priority for the winter transfer window, with Pochettino and the club’s hierarchy in agreement on the need for reinforcements.

The Red Devils have endured a difficult start to the campaign, and their attacking problems have become increasingly apparent since Benjamin Sesko’s injury. The Slovenian striker is expected to remain unavailable until January, leaving Pochettino short of reliable options in the final third.

Man City vs. Napoli team news: Maresca makes five changes, De Bruyne decision made for Champions League game

By Oliver Thomas | 24 Nov 2026 18:54, Last updated: 24 Nov 2026 19:11

Enzo Maresca has made a total of five changes to his Manchester City starting lineup for tonight’s Champions League clash against Napoli at the Etihad Stadium.

Matheus Nunes has recovered from an ankle injury and is fit to start, while Enzo Fernandez, Rayan Cherki, Josko Gvardiol and Allan also come into the side following last weekend’s 1-1 Premier League draw with Fulham.

Ruben Dias, Abdukodir Khusanov, Phil Foden, Iliman Ndiaye and Jeremy Doku all revert to the substitutes' bench for the visit of the Serie A side.

While Nunes will begin at right-back, Gvardiol is set to start at centre-back alongside Marc Guehi, with Nico O'Reilly retaining his spot at left-back.

Elliot Anderson and Ayyoub Bouaddi will continue to link arms in centre-midfield, with Fernandez expected to operate further forward in an advanced central role behind striker and captain Erling Haaland.

This could therefore see playmaker Rayan Cherki move out wide, with the Frenchman potentially occupying the left flank as Allan most likely begins on the right.

As for Napoli, head coach Massimiliano Allegri has made a total of three changes to the side that began the 1-0 win over Torino 04 in Serie A last weekend.

Kevin De Bruyne has been recalled to start against his former club for the second time since leaving in 2025. The legendary Belgian playmaker was sacrificed just 21 minutes into his first Etihad homecoming last season, following an early red card for captain Matteo Politano in a 2-0 defeat.

Defender Mathias Olivera and forward Noa Lang have also returned to Napoli’s first XI, with Rafa Marin, Andre-Frank Zambo Anguissa and Antonio Vergara all making way.

Scotland duo Billy Gilmour and Scott McTominay – who scored the match-winner against Torino – will both start in centre-midfield, while ex-Man United striker Rasmus Hojlund will be supported by Lang in attack.

Senegal KEEP Africa Cup of Nations title: Champions see crown restored in huge CAS verdict despite walking off the pitch in chaotic final against Morocco

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 09:30 EST, 24 November 2026 | Updated: 14:47 EST, 24 November 2026

Senegal have been reinstated as Africa Cup of Nations winners after succeeding with an appeal to the Court of Arbitration for Sport (CAS), ten months after they beat Morocco in Rabat.

The ruling follows a controversial final in which Senegal initially won 1-0 after extra time, only for the result to be overturned following an appeal to Confederation of African Football (CAF) by the Moroccan Football Federation.

Two months after the final, the CAF appeal board ruled that Senegal had forfeited the match because their players had walked off the pitch in protest over a controversial penalty decision.

Morocco were subsequently awarded a 3-0 victory and the title. Despite that, Senegal refused to hand the trophy over.

That decision was based on Articles 82 and 84 of the AFCON regulations, which state that leaving the pitch constitutes a forfeit.

However, following an appeal to CAS, Senegal have been reinstalled as champions, with the court ruling that FIFA's law that the referee’s decision is final takes precedence over the competition regulations.

Therefore, because the officials did not enforce the competition regulations at the time, there were no grounds to overturn the result.

The Moroccan FA, though, have not given up and have vowed to take the matter beyond CAS after questioning the integrity of panelist Ulrich Haas.

The Court of Arbitration for Sport has restored Senegal as Africa Cup of Nations winners after they were successful in their appeal

Morocco were awarded a 3-0 victory two months after the final, after it was ruled Senegal had forfeited the match by walking off the pitch in protest over a late penalty decision

Senegal fans clashed with security in Rabat after the controversial penalty decision

Ruling in favour of Senegal on Tuesday, CAS said: 'In the Panel’s view, there is no doubt that the effects of the Referee’s decision were limited to the pitch.

'As such, the Panel considers that the Referee’s decision was covered by the "field of play" doctrine.

'Since Articles 82 and 84 of the AFCON Regulations are intended solely to determine the outcome of a match when a team’s conduct leads the referee to call off the game permanently, they are therefore not applicable in this case.'

A statement from the Moroccan FA later added: 'We take note of the decision of the Court of Arbitration for Sport regarding the appeal filed by the Senegalese Football Federation against the decision of the CAF Appeals Committee, relating to the final match of the 2025 Africa Cup of Nations. In this context, the Royal Moroccan Football Federation expresses its surprise and protest at the failure to take into account its demand to arrange the necessary legal consequences for practices that would compromise the integrity and credibility of the arbitration procedure, especially what has been confirmed regarding communication between the German referee Ulrich Haas (who sat on the CAS panel) and the president of the Senegalese Football Federation, 48 hours before the decision was issued.

'The Royal Moroccan Football Federation also notes that the decision did not take into account the approach recently adopted by both the International Football Association Board (IFAB) and FIFA, regarding players leaving the field of play without the referee’s permission, and the resulting disciplinary responsibilities and penalties.

'Accordingly, the Royal Moroccan Football Federation confirms its intention to pursue all available legal avenues to appeal the decision of the Court of Arbitration for Sport.'

The chaos was triggered by events at the end of normal time, when referee Jean Jacques Ndala awarded Morocco a contentious penalty in the 98th minute after being called to the monitor by the video assistant referee to review El Hadji Malick Diouf’s challenge on Brahim Diaz.

The decision came just minutes after Senegal had seen a goal ruled out.

Furious with the penalty decision, Senegal manager Pape Thiaw instructed his players to leave the pitch in protest.

After a 17-minute delay, the team eventually returned, with former Liverpool forward Sadio Mane earning praise for remaining on the pitch and encouraging his teammates to continue.

In the dramatic aftermath, Real Madrid forward Brahim Diaz saw his Panenka penalty stopped by Senegal goalkeeper Edouard Mendy, before Ndala brought normal time to an end.

Senegal went on to score early in extra time, which has now led to a second title in the past three editions of the tournament with their 1-0 victory eventually confirmed in the courtroom.

Freddie Potts on the pain of leaving West Ham, why he had to move on this summer, upsetting the odds in the Champions League with Club Brugge - and why they don't fear Liverpool!

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 08:00 EST, 24 November 2026 | Updated: 14:15 EST, 24 November 2026

There are few transfers that get West Ham supporters talking quite like the sale of an academy graduate.

Who can forget the uproar after Grady Diangana joined West Brom in 2020, when club captain Mark Noble released a public statement voicing his anger and disappointment at the move.

Noble’s thoughts were echoed by team-mates at the time, while supporters were similarly up in arms at the sale of the winger.

So, when Freddie Potts joined Club Brugge for £10million on the eve of the season to mark the controversial departure of another product of the proclaimed Academy of Football, you can imagine the reaction.

Potts represented more than a graduate. A boyhood West Ham fan whose dad Steve is a club legend and still serves as a first-team coach, he joined the academy aged six. His brother Dan also came through the ranks.

The midfielder had just enjoyed a breakthrough campaign despite West Ham’s relegation, and many felt he was the perfect player to build their Championship promotion push around.

Freddie Potts is shining in Belgium with Club Brugge after joining from West Ham in the summer

Potts joined the Hammers academy aged six and enjoyed a breakthrough year last season

Hammers favourites Kevin Nolan and Tony Cottee weighed in with their disapproval at his sale, while celebrity fan Ray Winstone expressed his shock and anger live on talkSPORT.

‘Of course you see it (the division about the move) on social media,’ Potts tells Daily Mail Sport. ‘Every player does. But even though last season was tough I always felt I had a connection with the West Ham fans.

‘No matter if I played well or bad I always felt I understood what it meant to play for West Ham. It's a great club with great fans and people. In the end, I had to do what was best for me. I wanted to play this season, and I felt like this was the best place to do that.’

Potts, 22 at the time of his exit, admits it was extremely tough and emotional for him and his family to leave a club he had been at for 16 years.

But, during pre-season he became aware he wasn’t likely to play as much as he wanted to under Nuno Espirito Santo.

‘In the end it’s the manager’s decision,’ Potts says. ‘I wasn’t getting the minutes I wanted. The feeling was that I wasn’t guaranteed to be in the plans as much as Club Brugge were promising me, so I thought it was best for me to move.

‘It was very hard. West Ham have been my club my whole life and I have a lot of love for West Ham. But I had to take into account what was best for my career and I’m loving it at Brugge. It’s a really exciting place full of great people and a great manager.’

Potts had multiple options in the summer, including Premier League interest, yet the chance to play in the Champions League and compete for titles with Brugge attracted him.

Their record of developing young players and a talented squad that includes the likes of Carlos Forbs and Nicolo Tresoldi was equally compelling, and the midfielder hasn't looked back.

Potts started Brugge’s first 10 league games, missing their final one before the international break due to suspension. He returned at the weekend as they thrashed Mechelen 5-1. Ivan Leko’s side are one point adrift of Union Saint-Gilloise at the top of the Belgian Pro League, and they have also impressed in the Champions League.

In Europe, Brugge have beaten Inter Milan, PSV and Lens on their way to nine points from 12, only narrowly losing 3-2 to Aston Villa. Potts has started each match, including their memorable 4-3 win against Inter at the San Siro, and they host Liverpool on Wednesday.

‘It's a really good group of lads,’ he says. ‘Everyone speaks English as well which helps me. It's a good team and we're happy playing with each other.’

He is clearly enjoying life and is just the fifth English player in history to play for the club. Potts did take time to settle though, and admits he initially suffered from homesickness.

‘I found it difficult because East London and Essex have been my home for my whole life,’ he says. ‘West Ham was my life. It was tough in terms of moving into a new language and everything but you get used to it and I’m really enjoying it here. It’ll be strange going back home to England.'

Potts has been a regular and has had a key role in Brugge's impressive early exploits in Europe

Brugge have beaten PSV, Lens and Inter Milan, and they take on Liverpool this Wednesday

Potts’ family have popped over to see him and he has enjoyed exploring his new surroundings. He is yet to learn the local language and scolds himself for being lazy for relying on English, although he has picked up little bits of Flemish.

He has immersed himself in the culture too, and reveals he has tried the world-famous Belgian fries and a few local beers, albeit only when he has enough time to recover between games.

Despite his new life in Europe, Potts remains a West Ham fan and tries to watch them on TV every week.

He has been surprised by their struggles on their return to the Championship for the first time in 14 years, with Nuno paying the price by losing his job. Former Hammers midfielder Scott Parker has since taken the reins.

‘I still think they're playing well,’ Potts says. ‘They've got a great team and Scott Parker is a really good manager. You get ups and downs but I still 100 per cent think West Ham will get promoted this year.'

Potts’ stay in the Hammers first team may have been brief, yet there were still plenty of highs, not least a storming performance on his first Premier League start. That came in a win over Newcastle last November in the Hammers’ first home victory of the season. He was also coached by his dad, an experience he jokes had positives and negatives.

He left West Ham after realising he was unlikely to be first choice under Nuno Espirito Santo

Potts is a boyhood West Ham fan and is fully expecting them to get promoted this season

Attention now turns to Brugge’s clash against Liverpool. It promises to be a tricky challenge for Andoni Iraola’s side against a team who are determined to prove themselves on the European stage.

The two teams are level on points in the league phase, and Potts is keen for his side to take another scalp.

‘I don't think there's much difference in the way we've been performing in the Champions League,’ Potts concludes. ‘It'll be difficult for them to come to our place as well.

‘They're a great team, but we're going to approach it like we have with every Champions League game. We've gone away and beaten Milan, we've beaten PSV away. These are top teams.

‘Results like that give you a boost of how far you can go. You don't want to look too far ahead but every game in this competition is a chance to show everyone what you can do.

‘Liverpool will be a tough game but we've proved it against top teams before and we're going in thinking that we can do it again.’

&quot;He's been great for us. Ever since he joined us. He's …

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mack Baltes: Memphis Grizzlies head coach Tuomas Iisalo on Cameron Boozer and his impact: "He's been great for us. Ever since he joined us. Brandon Aiyuk landing spots: Commanders, Steelers among four potential fits for former 49ers star

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

Brandon Aiyuk is up for grabs after the San Francisco 49ers released Aiyuk on Monday, ending the duo's tumultuous relationship.

The NFL granted San Francisco a two-week roster spot exemption when the league reinstated him on Nov. 9, but with that exemption ending on Monday, the 49ers moved on. Aiyuk hasn't played in a game since his 2024 torn ACL and MCL, which means he never played a snap on his four-year, $120 million extension he signed right before the start of that season. That contract possesses a $40.5 million cap hit for 2027, so any team that wishes to acquire him will likely allow him to clear waivers first before signing him. The last full season Aiyuk played, San Francisco won the NFC and lost to the Kansas City Chiefs in overtime of the Super Bowl, with him playing like one of the league's best pass catchers.

Rate of catches for a first down or a touchdown

* The 2023 season was Aiyuk's last fully healthy season played

So which teams could be interested in the 28-year-old's services?

The Washington Commanders are the obvious landing spot. He has previously openly campaigned to join because of his connection with Jayden Daniels. They played together at Arizona State in 2019, Aiyuk's senior year and Daniels' freshman year, and Aiyuk racked up 1,192 yards receiving and eight touchdowns on 65 catches during their 12 games together.

Washington also has a glaring need at wide receiver. Terry McLaurin went down with a torn patellar tendon in his left knee, and veteran wide receiver Stefon Diggs, who is on a one-year contract, is the only player with more than two receiving touchdowns, six, on the Commanders this season. Aiyuk might behave himself for Daniels in Washington.

The Pittsburgh Steelers (6-4) are clinging to the AFC's No. 7 seed, the third and final wildcard spot, after consecutive losses. They face-planted against the Cincinnati Bengals in Week 10 and the Philadelphia Eagles in Week 11. Pittsburgh needs more offensive firepower ASAP. Aaron Rodgers would likely welcome all the help he can get, especially after hot-headed wide receiver DK Metcalf got himself ejected on Sunday. Metcalf and Aiyuk would be a handful, but Rodgers is one of the few quarterbacks who has the skins on the wall to get them in check.

If Aiyuk really wanted to stick it to the 49ers, joining the NFC West rival Los Angeles Rams would be one way to do it. The Rams are on the cusp of the NFC's No. 7 seed, but it will be a slog for them to actually reach the playoffs without their best two wide receivers available. Puka Nacua is expected to miss the rest of the 2026 season after undergoing surgery for an MCL injury in his knee. Davante Adams, who led the league with 14 receiving touchdowns in 2025, is still sidelined with a hamstring injury that could have him out for a few more weeks.

That means quarterback Matthew Stafford, who has only been around league average this season with the No. 15 passer rating (92.8) and No. 13 expected points added (EPA) per dropback (0.08), needs to rediscover his 2025 NFL MVP form ASAP. Having Aiyuk could help expedite that process.

There's a chance Drake Maye doesn't return to action this season because of the fibula injury he suffered in Week 10 against the Detroit Lions. However, the Patriots (5-5) are just a game out of the AFC playoff picture. Blockbuster trade acquisition A.J. Brown has been a bust so far, and Romeo Doubs (shoulder) is on injured reserve. If Mike Vrabel wants to do everything he can to make sure the defending AFC champs don't miss out on the 2026 postseason, signing Aiyuk could make some sense.

Dancing With the Stars co-host Julianne Hough is dating Peloton instructor Adrian Williams, just a few months after teasing on Live From E!: 2026 Emmys that she may not be dancing solo.

Julianne Hough is giving dating another waltz.

The Dancing With the Stars co-host is dating Peloton instructor Adrian Williams, a source confirmed to E! News.

The fitness instructor, 42, has already proved they're a winning pair by sharing a sweet glimpse into their bond on social media, posting a snap of Hough holding her dog Sunny on his Instagram Story Nov. 22, which she reshared.

E! News has reached out to reps for Hough and Williams for comment and has not yet heard back.

Though Hough has kept much of her love life private since she and Brooks Laich finalized their divorce in 2022, she did reveal that a new man might be lacing up his ballroom shoes.

"I might not be single," she teased E!'s Justin Sylvester at the 2026 Emmys in September. "There's a lot to discuss off-camera."

And the 38-year-old—who split from the former Washington Capitals player in 2020 after nearly three years of marriage—had previously spelled out what she's looking for in a partner.

"It's all energy-based," she told E! News in December 2025. "I don't really have a type. It's more connection, and that's it."

The former dance pro also knows that, when it comes to blossoming romances, it takes two to tango. As she put it, "I'm always looking for love, whether it's in a romantic sense or friends. Always."

As Hough cha-cha'd through her single era, she revealed her plan was pretty simple: Freestyle her love life.

"I've been very intentional about what I'm putting out and what I'm excited to attract and hopefully be attractive to," she explained to E!, "but I will say that I kind of just threw that out the window."

The attitude proved successful, as she shared how much she loved rediscovering herself.

"It brought me back to the joyful, playful side, versus being so serious," she reflected. "Sometimes, when you're intentional, you can get a little bit tunnel vision, and so, I kind of was like, 'You know what? I'm just gonna be playful and fun.'"

But she does have one caveat. Simply put, she's looking for "someone super hot."

NBA Cup 2026: Standings, schedule, stakes and clinching scenarios for final week of group play

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The final week of NBA Cup group play is here, with games on Tuesday, Wednesday and Friday. Only five teams have been eliminated so far, with 25 teams still battling for the eight spots in the knockout stage, which will take place Dec. 4-11.

Who will advance? Here's a look at the current standings, remaining schedule and stakes in each group.

Note: Ties will be broken by (in sequential order): head-to-head record in group play; point differential; total points scored; 2025-26 regular-season record; and, if necessary, a random drawing.

Magic, Raptors, Pistons can still win group

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Magic clinch group with a win, and clinch home-court advantage in quarterfinals with a win + Celtics or Heat loss

Pistons eliminated with: a loss + Wizards win; or a loss + Bucks win; or a loss + Heat and Cavs wins

Nets eliminated with: a Wizards win; or a Nets loss, or Heat and Cavs wins

Heat, Cavs, 76ers, Pacers, Knicks are all still alive

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Cavs eliminated with loss + Wizards and Heat wins; or various point-differential scenarios

76ers eliminated with loss + Wizards or Cavs win

Pacers eliminated with Wizards and Heat wins; or Wizards and Cavs wins; or Heat and Cavs wins

Knicks eliminated with a loss or Wizards and Heat wins

Wednesday: Miami at Philadelphia, 5 p.m. (ESPN)

Wednesday: Cleveland at New York, 7:30 p.m.

Friday: Miami at New York, 7 p.m. (Prime)

Friday: Philadelphia at Indiana, 7:30 p.m.

Celtics, Hawks, Wizards can still win group

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Wizards eliminated with: a loss + Hornets win; or a loss + Bulls tiebreaker win; or a loss + Heat and Cavs wins or Knicks win by seven or fewer points; or a loss by 18 or more points + Pistons and Nets wins

Bulls eliminated with: a loss; or a Wizards win; or tiebreaker loss to Wizards; or Heat and Cavs wins; or Pistons and Nets wins + a win by 13 or fewer points

Tuesday: Boston at Washington, 8 p.m. (NBC)

Mavericks, Nuggets, Suns can still win group

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Mavericks clinch group with a win and Suns loss

Suns are eliminated with: a loss + Mavs win; or a loss + Thunder and Grizzlies wins; or a loss + Warriors and Blazers wins

Rockets are eliminated with: a loss; or a Mavs win; or a win by four or fewer points; or Thunder and Grizzlies wins; or Warriors and Blazers wins

Timberwolves, Thunder, Grizzlies, Clippers still alive

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Timberwolves clinch group with a win + Grizzlies loss

Thunder clinch group with a win + Clippers loss

Thunder are eliminated with: a loss + Clippers win; or a loss + Nuggets and Suns wins; or a loss + Warriors and Blazers wins

Grizzlies are eliminated with: a loss + Wolves win; or a loss + Nuggets and Suns wins; or a loss + Warriors and Blazers wins

Clippers are eliminated with: a loss + Thunder win; or a loss + Nuggets and Suns wins; or a loss + Warriors and Blazers wins

Wednesday: Minnesota at Oklahoma City, 7:30 p.m. (ESPN)

Friday: New Orleans at LA Clippers, 9:30 p.m.

Spurs, Lakers, Blazers, Warriors still alive

Clinching scenarios for Tuesday and Wednesday

Blazers are eliminated with: a loss + Kings win; or a loss + Nuggets and Suns wins; or a loss + Thunder and Grizzlies wins

Warriors are eliminated with: a loss + Nuggets and Suns wins; or a loss + Thunder and Grizzlies wins

Wednesday: San Antonio at Portland, 10 p.m. (ESPN)

Wednesday: Sacramento at Golden State, 10 p.m.

Friday: LA Lakers at San Antonio, 9:30 p.m. (Prime)

Friday: Golden State at Portland, 9:30 p.m.

The eight teams that advance from group play will take part in a single-elimination tournament. The NBA Cup quarterfinals will be held on Dec. 4 and 5, with the semifinals on Dec. 8 and/or 9, with the higher-seeded teams hosting matchups in their home arenas. The top two teams will advance to the NBA Cup Final on Dec. 11.

Players of the teams that participate in the knockout stage will earn prize money.

(During the knockout stage, the 22 teams that didn't qualify will each play two to-be-determined, regular-season games.)

The NBA Cup is leaving Las Vegas, where the league has held the semifinals and championship game for the past three years. This year, the final will be held at Butler's Hinkle Fieldhouse in Indianapolis.

Courtois: ‘Leader’ Bellingham key to future success at Real Madrid

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Thibaut Courtois described Jude Bellingham as a "leader" on the pitch for Real Madrid, saying Los Blancos' chances of silverware this season may rest with the midfielder.

Courtois' comments came ahead of Madrid's Champions League clash with PSV on Tuesday, with Jose Mourinho's side winning all four of their games in Europe so far.

Madrid are on a nine-game winning run across all competitions, the latest of which came in dramatic fashion against Celta Vigo at the weekend, with Carlos Espi coming off the bench to save his team once again with an 85th-minute winner.

Bellingham was also on target in what was his 100th LaLiga appearance. He has now scored three goals in his last four games, having failed to net in his previous seven.

Since joining from Borussia Dortmund in 2023, Bellingham has played 182 times for Madrid in all competitions, scoring 60 goals as well as providing 41 assists.

He has six goals and three assists so far in 2026-27, and Courtois believes Bellingham is one of Madrid's most important players despite only being 23 years of age.

"Jude is a young guy but when you see him, it feels like he's a few years older," Courtois told reporters when asked about Bellingham's most impressive qualities.

"In the beginning, when you compare him with Arda [Guler], Arda was a quieter kid. With Jude, you already feel like he had a presence, but there isn't much difference in age.

"Jude is someone who really wants to win. That's why on the field he swears a lot! He's really a winner, and when he's not happy, he will make it known.

"He's also a leader on the field. He's a great player, a great mentality. You need those kinds of people like that in your team that go for everything.

"It seems like he has an engine to play 200 minutes. He keeps on going, running with the ball, it's really hard to dispossess him.

"And on the outside, he just works hard, he goes to the gym. With a good Jude, we play better."

NEXT MATCH @PSV 24/11 | 21:00 CET https://t.co/LcVCq5uN5v

— Real Madrid C.F. (@realmadriden) November 22, 2026

It was confirmed earlier this week that Courtois had agreed to fresh terms in the Spanish capital, signing a new deal that will keep him with Madrid until the summer of 2028.

"I'm very proud and happy," Courtois said. "My objective is to end my career here. I want to keep playing and stay healthy. I'm grateful for the club's confidence in me."

And Courtois will be hoping to play his part against PSV in the Champions League, with this the first meeting between the two sides in 37 years.

Under Mourinho, Real have won 17 of their 20 Champions League matches at the Estadio Bernabeu (D2 L1), with their only defeat coming against Barcelona in the 2010-11 semi-final first leg. It's the best home win percentage (85%) for a manager at a club (min. 20 games) in Champions League history.

"We've had to find how the boss wanted to play, to understand each other better," Courtois said of Mourinho's start to life back in Madrid.

"Our two defeats, against [Real] Betis we didn't deserve to lose, against Atletico [Madrid] the circumstances that led to that result.

"I knew [Mourinho] when I was 22, that was a long time ago. He has a lot more experience. Over the years you change a bit.

"He's calm, and when he has to speak clearly he speaks clearly. He's a great boss. As a team, we want to keep winning and lift trophies at the end of the season."

Brentford 4-0 Everton: David Moyes' record-breaking night ends in misery as James Tarkowski sees red and rampant Bees boost European hopes

📅 2026-11-23  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Published: 17:31 EST, 23 November 2026 | Updated: 18:06 EST, 23 November 2026

David Moyes’ record-breaking night as Everton manager could not have been more miserable.

In his 593rd game as Toffees boss, Moyes broke Harry Catterick’s 75-year record for most games in the dugout.

But that was about the only positive he could take away from the Gtech Community Stadium, as high-flying Brentford battered ten-man Everton on an evening to forget for the 63-year-old Scotsman.

Daily Mail Sport's Bobby Manzi was at the Gtech Community Stadium on a torrid night for the Toffees...

Brentford, having missed out on European football on goal difference last season, are showing all the signs of being there or thereabouts again.

They climbed to sixth, leapfrogging Leeds — who have had far more praise lavished on them this season than Keith Andrews’ reliable side.

Brentford are disciplined with plenty of attacking flair, even with frontman and top goalscorer Igor Thiago suspended.

In his 593rd game as Toffees boss, Moyes broke Harry Catterick’s 75-year record

Brentford, having missed out on European football on goal difference last season, are showing all the signs of being there or thereabouts again

The Premier League lacks quality this season, but Brentford are consistent, losing just twice.

This well-oiled Andrews machine must take advantage of the floundering elite, with Manchester United, Chelsea and Tottenham all struggling, and finally secure Europe.

‘We set out this season looking to be better than last season, and if that happens, we will be flying to different destinations next season,’ Andrews said of their European hopes.’

‘We've got a very competitive squad that strives to get better. If we continue with that mindset, we'll hopefully be in a good place.

‘I enjoyed us,’ he added about the performance. ‘I thought we were very good. We played with real bravery, courage and energy.’

Brentford limited Everton to just three touches in the penalty area in the first half. They grabbed the game by the scruff of the neck and pinned their opponents back.

Jaidon Anthony was superb on the flank, providing two assists. Midfielder Vitaly Janelt capped off a brilliant display with a wonder strike from 25 yards, finding the top corner with his left foot.

It was a surprise to see Kevin Schade benched but this team is so cohesive, with more focus on the unit than any individual stars, that victory was light work for them.

It was a surprise to see Kevin Schade (above) benched but this team is so cohesive, with more focus on the unit than any individual stars, that victory was light work for them

Brentford: Kelleher 7; Kayode 7, Ajer 7, van den Berg 7, Lewis-Potter 9 (Diouf 74min, 6); Yarmolyuk 8 (Jensen 74, 7), Janelt 8 (Sangare 82); Ouattara 6 (Schade 66, 6), Damsgaard 7, Anthony 8 (Carvalho 81, 7); Wilson 7.

Everton: Pickford 5; O’Brien 5, Tarkowski 4, Branthwaite 5, Mykolenko 5; Armstrong 5, Garner 6 (Norgaard 85); Rohl 5 (Keane 69, 6), Dewsbury-Hall 5 (Hackney 69, 6), George 5 (Grealish 66, 5); Barry 4 (Johnson 69, 5).

Both of Keane Lewis-Potter’s goals had an element of fortune attached. But teams earn that luck when they manage ten shots in their opponents’ penalty area in just 45 minutes.

Lewis Potter starred at left-back, registering seven shots — the most of any player — and the joint-most touches in the opposition box. He was a menace and integral to every Brentford attack.

James Tarkowski endured a depressing return to his former club when he was shown the first Premier League red card of his career.

It will split opinion, but in this day and age it is a clear red card. Although the Everton defender won the ball, his studs connected with Yehor Yarmolyuk’s shin on follow-through. The speed and intensity of the tackle endangered his opponent. He had to go.

Referee Craig Pawson brandished a yellow, but a quick trip to the monitor saw it upgraded. Tarkowski ran down the tunnel before Pawson could even get the red card out of his pocket.

The home support enjoyed it, having taunted their former player throughout the first half.

To compound Everton’s misery, the first game of Tarkowski three-match ban is next weekend’s Merseyside derby.

Although Tarkowski won the ball, his studs connected with Yarmolyuk’s shin on follow-through

A deflated Moyes had few complaints. ‘My feeling at the time was it wasn’t a red because James easily got to the ball before the Brentford player,’ the Everton boss said.

‘But after I saw it again, Tarky's boot is a bit higher than I'd like, so I can understand why the decision went against us.

‘We didn’t play well. Brentford did and credit to them. A bit unfortunate with the goals, it made it look even worse than it was.’

Everton were unbeaten in four games at Brentford before this and their defeat came in the form of the sublime and the ridiculous.

The first goal was all about Anthony undoing Everton on the left before Lewis-Potter was given far too much space to get his shot away. The deflection was unfortunate, but the defending was poor to begin with.

Tarkowski blocked an initial effort from Lewis-Potter for the second goal but the ball fell straight back to him, and Jake O’Brien helped his second attempt past Jordan Pickford. Once again, Everton should have done better and cleared the initial free-kick.

Portuguese forward Fabio Carvalho could not believe his luck when he added a late fourth, tapping in as Mathias Jensen’s corner flew straight past the congested pack at the near post.

Ethan Nwaneri: Mikel Arteta sends Arsenal loan star warning ahead of Champions League reunion

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Your weekly briefing on Arsenal, featuring team news and expert analysis from Matt Verri

I would like to be emailed about offers, event and updates from Evening Standard. Read our privacy notice.

Mikel Arteta has warned Ethan Nwaneri that "moments are not enough" if he is to turn his Borussia Dortmund loan around.

The young forward joined the German club in the summer on loan from Arsenal and he was initially handed opportunities in the starting lineup by Niko Kovac.

However, those have been few and far between in recent weeks. Nwaneri has not started a match in over a month and has played just five minutes of football across Dortmund's last three league matches.

The German side, who sit top of the Bundesliga, travel to north London to take on the Premier League champions on Tuesday in the Champions League and Nwaneri is eligible to face his parent club.

It appears unlikely, though, that the 19-year-old will start against the Gunners considering Dortmund's fine form, and Arteta has warned the teenager that nothing will come easy.

"We're talking here about the highest level in Europe," Arteta said.

"To play at that level is extremely difficult. You can have patches, but to find that consistency and to play a big amount of minutes and have a huge impact at such big football clubs like Arsenal and Dortmund is extremely difficult.

"That's the reality. You can just shine in moments, but moments are not enough. Now you need to show the consistency.

"Ethan has the capacity and the mentality to do that but it takes some time."

Arteta added: "He needs to earn the right to play. He's playing at the top of the Bundesliga and the players have huge talent and quality and they are showing that and that's the reality of football.

"You go there and because you come from Arsenal, it doesn't give you any advantage, so you need to earn it.

"Ethan has, first of all, the mindset to do it and then the quality to do it and these things are part of a journey and part of a season and he needs to reveal himself and earn the right to win."

Nwaneri spent the second half of last season on loan at Marseille and barely played after Roberto De Zerbi's departure in February.

Arsenal are understood to have the option to recall Nwaneri in January if his loan at Dortmund is proving to be similarly unproductive.

Arsenal injury update: Raya, Saka and Zubimendi latest return dates

Transfer news LIVE: Shock Arsenal move; Man Utd striker deal

Guimaraes reveals Arsenal ‘apology’ after £75m transfer

For now, though, Arteta remains "unconvinced" that the move can still benefit Nwaneri's development.

"He was in Marseille as well," the Spaniard said.

"In Marseille he had a completely different context for a lot of things that are so unexpected to happen.

"But that builds a player and builds the character. I'm so convinced of that. You are about to fulfil a role that you are given on the day and try and change that for the one you actually want. That's football."

Chelsea are reportedly keeping a close watch on Everton midfielder Harrison Armstrong, with Arsenal and Manchester City also interested in the highly-rated youngster ahead of the upcoming transfer windows.

The 19-year-old has enjoyed an impressive breakthrough campaign at Goodison Park, attracting attention from several Premier League clubs with his performances in David Moyes’ side.

According to a report from Fussball News, Chelsea are keen to monitor Armstrong’s progress and have been receiving regular scouting reports on the England Under-21 international. The Blues could consider making a move for the midfielder during the January transfer window.

Armstrong has established himself as one of Everton’s most promising young players, with his physical presence, energy and technical ability making him an exciting prospect for the future.

His versatility is another attractive quality, as the teenager can contribute in different roles across central midfield. His ability to combine defensive responsibilities with forward contributions could make him a valuable addition to Chelsea’s squad.

However, the London club will face competition from Arsenal and Manchester City, who are also monitoring the youngster’s development. Both clubs have a reputation for investing in talented young players, which could make the race for his signature increasingly difficult.

The midfielder has also earned three appearances for England’s Under-21 side, further highlighting his growing reputation.

Want more CaughtOffside coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust.

Everton are under no immediate pressure to sell their academy graduate, with Armstrong under contract until June 2028.

The Toffees reportedly value the teenager at between €60 million and €70 million, a significant increase following his impressive progress this season. Any potential deal could also involve additional performance-related bonuses.

Chelsea could therefore face a substantial financial commitment if they decide to formalise their interest.

There is currently no indication that an agreement is close, with Arsenal and Manchester City also assessing the situation.

A January departure remains uncertain, but Everton could struggle to retain Armstrong if his performances keep attracting attention from England’s biggest clubs.

Bayern Munich can’t stand Jamal Musiala’s situation any longer!

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Bayern Munich had a very difficult evening when they received Cologne (1-1). Harry Kane came off with a serious ankle injury after 20 minutes. At the break, it was Michael Olise who had to give up his place, also affected, less severely, but he still left the stadium on crutches. For the Frenchman, we do not yet know his length of unavailability but it should not be too long, compared to the Ballon d’Or. The Englishman will have to be absent for several months after rupturing an ankle ligament.

Jamal Musiala played the last 20 minutes of the game. He is still facing health problems, he who suffers from a neurological dysfunction preventing him from being able to last the length of a match. Problem, he is one of the highest paid players at the club and his situation is starting to get talked about. Until then, he had always received the full support of his management but the first flaws appeared last night with a vitriolic interview with Karl-Heinz Rummenigge. The latter let go a little about the future of the 23-year-old player.

To summarize, the Bavarian legend considers that this situation is no longer tenable. “It’s entirely possible (that he regains his level, editor’s note) and we cannot rule it out. Unfortunately, what we know suggests that it will be difficult to reach the level he has so often displayed here.immediately hits the member of the supervisory board, and former chairman of the club’s board of directors, before continuing. “We need to talk to the family, the player and especially his representatives. These sums are no longer possible – sorry, with all due respect.»

The former striker continues his explanation by highlighting the interests of the club. “I can’t imagine Jamal leaving the club or another club buying him in these circumstances. This is problematic. We handled the situation very tactfully with the player, and we all want him back, ideally at the top of his form. But what will happen if this is not the case?he asks. It will then be necessary to look at the financial aspects. And this is a task that unfortunately falls to the board of directors and the supervisory board.»

A meeting will soon take place, Rummenigge promises, between Bayern, Musiala, who is under contract until 2030, and his representatives to try to find a solution. “We prefer to wait and observe how the situation develops in the coming weeks. We will then have to carefully monitor events before making a decision.» Asked about these statements after last night’s draw, Vincent Kompany preferred to kick into touch. “If Jamal regains his level and form, and if we achieve it together, FC Bayern will come out on top.»

Five-time Solheim Cup player Khang secures LPGA Tour card for 2027

📅 Nov 23, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The top-25 finishers and ties at the 2026 LPGA Q-Series final qualifying stage have been awarded LPGA Tour status for 2027; Megha Ganne won the event outright, while Megan Khang - part of Team USA for the past five Solheim Cups - is one of 27 players to claim a tour card for next season

Megha Ganne won the 2026 LPGA Q-Series final qualifying stage as 27 tour cards were claimed for the 2027 season, with five-time USA Solheim Cup team member Megan Khang among those successful.

Khang - part of Team USA for the past five Solheim Cups - is returning to the LPGA Tour, on which she's made 259 starts since 2016, winning the 2023 CPKC Women's Open.

The top-25 finishers and ties at the conclusion of the 2026 LPGA Q-Series final qualifying were awarded LPGA Tour status for next year.

American Ganne won the final stage outright on 23 under, one shot clear of Shannon Tan and Julia Gregg in a tie for second. Min Sun7 Kim was fourth on 21 under and Xiaowen Yin one further back in fifth.

Ganne, the 2025 US Amateur champion, had actually already secured her 2027 LPGA Tour status by entering the week by finishing 11-15 on the Epson Tour.

Gregg, who finished in a share of second, has never previously had a start on the LPGA Tour.

"It feels unreal," the 25-year-old American said after clinching her place. "It's been my dream since I was four years old to play on the LPGA and now that I finally did it."

The WTGL season continues on Wednesday when Lottie Woad's Boston Common chase their first win of the campaign against Charley Hull's Atlanta Drive GC. Live coverage begins on Wednesday from 1am on Sky Sports Golf.

The 2027 LPGA Tour season begins with the Hilton Grand Vacations Tournament of Champions from February 4-7. Get the latest Sky Sports deals or stream with no contract.

PSG's Doue to have surgery on ruptured ACL

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Desire Doue has scored six goals in all competitions for PSG this season

Paris St-Germain forward Desire Doue will have surgery later this month after rupturing the anterior cruciate ligament in his right knee.

The 21-year-old was in tears as he was taken off on a stretcher towards the end of the first half in the Ligue 1 champions' 3-2 victory over Nice on Saturday night.

Doue, who has arguably been PSG's best player this season, twisted awkwardly and went down under no contact.

The match was stopped for five minutes as Doue - who was visibly in pain - received treatment before he was applauded off by the crowd at the Allianz Riviera Stadium.

It is a huge set-back for the France attacker, who missed 13 games in 2025-26 with calf and hamstring injuries.

The European champions have been plagued with injury issues so far this season and are already without Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, who sustained an ACL injury of his own against Barcelona in October.

PSG return to action on Wednesday, when they host Roma in the Champions League.

Liverpool v Manchester United: Premier League – live

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Premier League updates from the 4.30pm GMT kick-off

Live scores | Latest table | And follow us over on TikTok

Everybody knows the pattern. Performances fade. Results falter. A title challenge becomes clinging on for the last Champions League spot becomes mid-table. Or a promising start becomes a troubling dip, becomes a full-on battle against relegation.

Fans begin to grumble. The media wonder whether the spark has gone. Players look disaffected. A dressing room, once lost, is seldom rediscovered, and few managers are given the opportunity to find it again. A crisis in football, once acknowledged, is only ever resolved in one way: by the sacking of the manager.

With hindsight, Michael Carrick’s dismissal was probably inevitable from the moment Manchester United lost at Hull on the opening weekend, just as Ruben Amorim was doomed from the moment cameras picked him up fiddling with magnetic counters on a tactics board in the rain at Grimsby. The message for Mauricio Pochettino is clear: don’t go playing badly on the east coast.

It’s generally acknowledged that Carrick, while he may not have been the ideal manager for United, was a long way down the list of the biggest problems at the club. So what was the point of sacking him? As Pochettino begins his United career at Anfield on Sunday, what realistically can any manager hope to achieve?

Mauricio Pochettino has called on Manchester United to have “no fear” when they travel to Anfield for his first game. The Argentinian does not want his players to take a backward step as he targets a fast start.

United are 19th in the Premier League and Liverpool fifth but the head coach will not accept any self-doubt. Pochettino is known for being attack-minded and is keen to instil those ideals, but will have had only three days with the players since replacing Michael Carrick.

“We are going to Anfield with the idea to win and be brave and to be Manchester United,” Pochettino told the club’s website. “The history talks for us, and what we need to expect. We need to be sure that we are brave and we are protagonists and go there with no fears and challenge a very good team like Liverpool.”

No big surprises in Mauricio Pochettino’s first Man Utd XI. The most notable selection is probably the centre-back pairing of Matthijs de Ligt (starting his first Premier League game since November 2025) and Lisandro Martinez.

There are three changes from the 4-4 draw with Aston Villa in Michael Carrick’s final game: De Ligt, Martinez and Patrick Dorgu replace Noussair Mazraoui, Harry Maguire and Amad Diallo.

Liverpool bring in Rio Ngumoha for Cody Gakpo, the only change from their win at Crystal Palace before the international break.

Liverpool (4-2-3-1) Alisson; Araujo, Jacquet, Van Dijk, Kerkez; Szoboszlai, Mac Allister; Ngumoha, Wirtz, Barcola; Isak.

Subs: Mamardashvili, Gomez, Chiesa, Tsimikas, Munoz, Frimpong, Nyoni, McConnell, Koumas.

Man Utd (4-2-3-1) Lammens; Yoro, De Ligt, Martinez, Dalot; Tielemans, Mainoo; Mbeumo, Fernandes, Dorgu; Cunha.

Subs: Mazraoui, Maguire, Mount, Zirkzee, Diallo, Andrey Santos, Heaten, Heaven, Lacey.

The first day in a new job is supposed to be gentle and relaxed: small talk, smaller admin, a few bits of work and some lamentably awkward handshakes. Mauricio Pochettino’s first day* as Manchester United manager is the polar opposite. Liverpool away, the ultimate domestic challenge for any United manager.

This quirk of employment opportunity and fixture list might be a good thing for Pochettino. It’s a free hit, with nobody of sound mind expecting his brave new world to begin until he has had months on the training ground. And if United do win, it’ll increase the length of the free pass that every new manager receives.

Andoni Iraola only joined Liverpool in June, yet he’s already their 11th longest-serving manager. Iraola has made a promising start, including some rousing victories in Europe in particular.

In the Premier League, Liverpool’s main problem has been their home form. They’ve taken five points from five games, but it’s a bit early to lament the demise of Fortress Anfield. Three of those five matches were against the top three of Manchester City, Arsenal and Brighton.

Today, almost unbelievably, Liverpool are playing the team in 19th place: Mauricio Pochettino’s Manchester United.

* Yes, yes, I know this isn’t actually his first day in the job, but you know exactly what I mean so let’s not allow semantics to undermine a perfectly half-arsed preamble.

→ Baca artikel asli

Mauricio Pochettino has already had one wish granted as Manchester United manager

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mauricio Pochettino has already had one of his first wishes granted as Manchester United manager.

The Argentine arrived in the north west on Wednesday, immediately boarding a plane from Cincinnati after his final match in charge of the United States national team to begin work at Carrington as soon as he touched down.

The former Tottenham and Chelsea head coach takes his new side to Anfield this afternoon to face Liverpool in a tricky first match at the helm.

The 54-year-old revealed in his introductory press conference on Friday that he wanted input in big decisions at United as his tenure wore on, and he has already had one particular request granted in his short spell in the hotseat.

In a round of quickfire questions with the club’s media team upon his arrival, Pochettino was asked whether he preferred team lineups to be formatted in shirt number or position order, with clubs having differing approaches to their pre-match graphics.

United have traditionally plumped for a shirt number-first approach, but when Pochettino’s first team selection was revealed ahead of the game against Liverpool, the Red Devils’ first XI was listed in position order.

It outlined that their new boss had gone for a classic 4-2-3-1 shape, listing Leny Yoro and Diogo Dalot as full-backs, and indicating that Matheus Cunha would play up front rather than Bryan Mbeumo.

United travel to Merseyside this afternoon mired in the Premier League relegation zone, winning just once in their last 11 games.

It was a streak that lost Michael Carrick his job, with the former midfielder sacked a week and a half ago after 303 days in charge.

Marc Marquez overtakes Valentino Rossi - now only Angel Nieto and Giacomo Agostini have more titles

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Marc Marquez continues to cement his legacy in motorcycle racing and sport as a whole. If his return to the top in 2025, with an impeccable season until he was injured, already left everyone stunned, the legendary Cervera rider has pushed his limits once again in 2026, overcoming every obstacle thrown his way to claim his 10th world title, and his eighth in MotoGP.

This is a tally that allows the Spaniard to continue breaking historical records. While many already consider him the greatest of all time, the #93 continues to strengthen his case with the numbers to back it up. And with the crown he secured this weekend at the Portuguese Grand Prix, he has reached milestones that many considered impossible in the modern era.

One of those is the number of motorcycle racing world titles across all categories. Marquez has reached the landmark of 10 championships, moving ahead of several legends of the sport.

With his 10 titles, Marquez has finally overtaken Valentino Rossi, who finished his career with nine, with his best opportunity to surpass that tally coming in 2015, when he ultimately lost out to Jorge Lorenzo amid the controversy surrounding Marc. Not only has Marquez surpassed the Doctor's tally, but also that of Mike 'The Bike' Hailwood, who won nine titles in the 1960s, and Italian Carlo Ubbiali, who achieved the same feat in the 1950s.

As a result, Marquez now has only two legendary figures ahead of him: Angel Nieto and Giacomo Agostini. Nieto's '12+1' world titles appeared untouchable until just a few years ago, but the reality is that Marquez is now just three championships short of the man regarded as the father of Spanish motorcycle racing.

It is worth remembering that Marquez still has at least two more years on his contract with Ducati, meaning he could potentially get very close to Nieto's tally. However, catching Agostini at the top of the all-time list, with his formidable 15 world titles, will be a much tougher task.

It should also be noted that during the eras of these great champions, it was common for riders to compete in more than one category in the same season, which explains the higher championship tallies. By contrast, legends such as Marquez and Rossi, as well as more recent champions with fewer titles, like Mick Doohan and Jorge Lorenzo, have competed primarily in a single class each season.

The picture changes considerably if we consider only world titles won in the premier class (500cc initially, followed by MotoGP from 2002). In that list, Marquez is already among the most successful riders in history, tied at the top with Giacomo Agostini. If Marquez wins another MotoGP title, he will become the most successful rider in the history of the premier class.

8 MotoGP titles  (2013, 2014, 2016-2019, 2025 and 2026)

2 500cc titles  (2001 and 2002), 5 MotoGP titles (2003-2005, 2008 and 2009)

4 500cc titles (1984, 1986, 1988 and 1989)

Liverpool vs Manchester United LIVE: Pochettino names team for first game as Premier League rivals face off

📅 Nov 22, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The headline act of this weekend’s Premier League action sees Mauricio Pochettino handed a baptism of fire at Anfield. The former Tottenham and Chelsea manager is back in the English top flight, becoming the latest man tasked with guiding Man Utd back to the top of the game after replacing Michael Carrick on a two-and-a-half-year deal.

Liverpool first up is a less-than-ideal start for Pochettino, who started his new role just four days ago and took charge of training for the first time just three days ago, footage of which was posted - and then deleted - off YouTube after being inadvertently captured by a drone. Marcus Rashford was then ruled out due to an ongoing injury issue.

So, it’s not been the best preparation for one of Man Utd’s biggest games of the season, while Liverpool have been boosted by the return to fitness of three stars, including captain Virgil van Dijk. Follow the game LIVE below with our dedicated match blog, featuring expert insight and analysis as Pochettino begins life at Man Utd.

Calvin Bassey has confirmed he is in talks over a new contract with Fulham, amid interest from Manchester United.

As reported by Standard Sport earlier this week, 26-year-old centre-back Bassey, was expected to be a transfer target for Mauricio Pochettino in the January transfer window.

Defence is a key area to strengthen for Man Utd, and Fulham captain Bassey has an admirer in Pochettino.

But Bassey, who is in the third year of a four-year contract at Fulham, is happy in west London and ready to commit his future to the club.

Mauricio Pochettino has already had one of his first wishes as Manchester United manager.

The 54-year-old revealed in his introductory press conference on Friday that he wanted input in big decisions at United as his tenure wore on, and he has already had one particular request granted in his short spell in the hotseat.

In a round of quickfire questions with the club’s media team upon his arrival, Pochettino was asked whether he preferred team lineups to be formatted in shirt number or position order, with clubs having differing approaches to their pre-match graphics.

United have traditionally plumped for a shirt number-first approach, but when Pochettino’s first team selection was revealed ahead of the game against Liverpool, the Red Devils’ first XI was listed in position order.

Andoni Iraola has sprung a surprise on his left flank too, with Cody Gakpo out of the matchday squad and Rio Ngumoha in from the off.

Virgil van Dijk is back to full fitness, and Ronald Araujo gets the nod at right-back ahead of the returning Jeremie Frimpong.

As expected from the Argentine, to be honest. Not much he can really do with that crop of players.

Senne Lammens in goal, and an interesting choice to bring Matthijs de Ligt straight in for Harry Maguire, but it is full-back that is still the real problem area with Luke Shaw still injured.

Diogo Dalot is at left-back once more and Leny Yoro fills in on the right once more.

In the engine room, Kobbie Mainoo and Youri Tielemans get the nod with Andrey Santos still struggling for starts, while Patrick Dorgu is the go-to left winger in the absence of Marcus Rashford.

Matheus Cunha leads the line, with Joshua Zirkzee still forced to settle for a place on the bench despite the change in manager.

Bruno Fernandes will lead Manchester United this afternoon, but talks over a new contract have ended after two rejected offers.

The Man Utd skipper has explained the reasoning behind his decision to Sky Sports ahead of today’s game.

Liverpool manager Andoni Iraola expects a much tougher test from Manchester United following the appointment of Mauricio Pochettino.

“What people think the league table tells us in this moment is not something that we can afford to be distracted by. This is a day when this kind of thing does not matter in the same way that it might otherwise. The history of the rivalry between our two clubs tells us that.

“We should expect and prepare for the best version of United because they will definitely see this fixture as an opportunity to start showing the best of themselves, particularly under new management.”

The operator, behind the YouTube account CC Overview, filmed around 12 minutes of footage at Carrington on Thursday, showing Pochettino working with his new squad ahead of Sunday’s Premier League trip to Liverpool.

It has led to suggestions on social media that Pochettino’s surprise starting line-up at Anfield has been leaked, with out-of-favour Joshua Zirkzee starting up top and Bryan Mbeumo playing in a wing-back role.
